[Initiative Order]\nBattles in this game use a timeline turn system. Once battle begins, every character is placed in the <color\=#FF0000>initiative order</color> according to their <color\=#FF0000>Speed</color> attribute. When a character reaches the front of the <color\=#FF0000>initiative order</color>, they may take an <color\=#FF0000>action</color>.=[Initiative Order]\nBattles in this game use a timeline turn system. Once battle begins, every character is placed in the <color\=#FF0000>initiative order</color> according to their <color\=#FF0000>Speed</color> attribute. When a character reaches the front of the <color\=#FF0000>initiative order</color>, they may take an <color\=#FF0000>action</color>.
[Inner Energy]\nTechniques cost Inner Energy, as set by each technique's <color\=#FF0000>Inner Energy cost</color>. Without enough Inner Energy a technique cannot be used, and it is shown darkened on the skill bar.=[Inner Energy]\nTechniques cost Inner Energy, as set by each technique's <color\=#FF0000>Inner Energy cost</color>. Without enough Inner Energy a technique cannot be used, and it is shown darkened on the skill bar.
[Techniques]\n<color\=#FF0000>Techniques</color> are the main actions in the game. Depending on their nature they can attack, heal, hinder and more.=[Techniques]\n<color\=#FF0000>Techniques</color> are the main actions in the game. Depending on their nature they can attack, heal, hinder and more.
Note that once you perform an <color\=#FF0000>action</color>, that turn ends automatically and the character loses any remaining <color\=#FF0000>movement squares</color>.=Note that once you perform an <color\=#FF0000>action</color>, that turn ends automatically and the character loses any remaining <color\=#FF0000>movement squares</color>.
[Actions]\nIn each round, a character receives one <color\=#FF0000>action</color>. Carrying out any command on the <color\=#FF0000>Action Bar</color> uses up that <color\=#FF0000>action</color>.=[Actions]\nIn each round, a character receives one <color\=#FF0000>action</color>. Carrying out any command on the <color\=#FF0000>Action Bar</color> uses up that <color\=#FF0000>action</color>.
[Movement]\nAt the start of an action, the character's <color\=#FF0000>movement range</color> is shown on screen according to their <color\=#FF0000>Movement</color> attribute. Left-click any square within range to move there; <color\=#FF0000>right-click</color> to return to the original position.=[Movement]\nAt the start of an action, the character's <color\=#FF0000>movement range</color> is shown on screen according to their <color\=#FF0000>Movement</color> attribute. Left-click any square within range to move there; <color\=#FF0000>right-click</color> to return to the original position.
[Hit and Evasion]\nWhen a character attacks, the <color\=#FF0000>Precision</color> and <color\=#FF0000>Evasion</color> of both sides determine <color\=#FF0000>whether it hits</color>. A successful <color\=#FF0000>evasion</color> ignores the attack's effects entirely.=[Hit and Evasion]\nWhen a character attacks, the <color\=#FF0000>Precision</color> and <color\=#FF0000>Evasion</color> of both sides determine <color\=#FF0000>whether it hits</color>. A successful <color\=#FF0000>evasion</color> ignores the attack's effects entirely.
[Vitality]\nWhen a character is attacked, their <color\=#FF0000>Vitality</color> falls. When <color\=#FF0000>Vitality</color> reaches zero, the unit can no longer fight and leaves the battle.=[Vitality]\nWhen a character is attacked, their <color\=#FF0000>Vitality</color> falls. When <color\=#FF0000>Vitality</color> reaches zero, the unit can no longer fight and leaves the battle.
[Time Cost]\nEvery action in the game has its own <color\=#FF0000>Time Cost</color>. After performing an action, this value affects the character's own <color\=#FF0000>place in the initiative order</color>.=[Time Cost]\nEvery action in the game has its own <color\=#FF0000>Time Cost</color>. After performing an action, this value affects the character's own <color\=#FF0000>place in the initiative order</color>.

[Secondary Objectives]\nBesides the usual conditions, some battles have <color\=#FF0000>secondary objectives</color>. They do not affect the outcome, but completing them earns extra rewards, so they are well worth attempting.=[Secondary Objectives]\nBesides the usual conditions, some battles have <color\=#FF0000>secondary objectives</color>. They do not affect the outcome, but completing them earns extra rewards, so they are well worth attempting.

Ten years ago the Wild Goose Peak Incident devastated the jianghu; ten years later, a treasure map has surfaced, from Yan Nantian, the Sword God who vanished on the eve of the incident. Renyi Manor meant to use the fragment it found to invite worthy men of the world to a Heroes' Banquet and reopen the Wild Goose Peak affair, but on the eve of the banquet, the manor's eldest senior sister stole the fragment and fled to Villains' Valley. By Leng Er's order, and against the late master's dying wish, keep a low profile, avoid trouble, and go to Villains' Valley to find your senior sister and recover the fragment.=Ten years ago the Wild Goose Peak Incident devastated the jianghu; ten years later, a treasure map has surfaced, from Yan Nantian, the Sword God who vanished on the eve of the incident. By Leng Er's order, and against the late master's dying wish, keep a low profile, avoid trouble, and go to Villains' Valley to find your senior sister and recover the fragment.

Deductions & Conclusions=Deductions & Conclusions
Several inferences lead to a conclusion, but during deduction you may reach a <color\=#FF0000>wrong inference</color>. A wrong inference leads to a <color\=#FF0000>wrong conclusion</color>, or <color\=#FF0000>no conclusion at all</color>. If so, click an inference in the Book of Deliberation that has <color\=#FF0000>alternative</color> readings and <color\=#FF0000>switch inferences</color> to reach a different conclusion and revise your deduction.=Several inferences lead to a conclusion, but during deduction you may reach a <color\=#FF0000>wrong inference</color>. A wrong inference leads to a <color\=#FF0000>wrong conclusion</color>, or <color\=#FF0000>no conclusion at all</color>. [Parry]\nWhen a character is attacked, there is a chance to trigger a <color\=#FF0000>Parry</color>, reducing the damage taken. The chance to parry is determined by the character's <color\=#FF0000>Parry</color> attribute, and the amount reduced by the <color\=#FF0000>Parry Reduction</color> attribute.=[Parry]\nWhen a character is attacked, there is a chance to trigger a <color\=#FF0000>Parry</color>, reducing the damage taken. The chance to parry is determined by the character's <color\=#FF0000>Parry</color> attribute, and the amount reduced by the <color\=#FF0000>Parry Reduction</color> attribute.
Counter Techniques=Counter Techniques
[Counter]\nBesides parrying, a character who is attacked also has a chance to trigger a <color\=#FF0000>Counter</color>. When a counter triggers, the defender <color\=#FF0000>ignores the initiative order</color> and uses a <color\=#FF0000>counter technique</color> against the attacker.=[Counter]\nBesides parrying, a character who is attacked also has a chance to trigger a <color\=#FF0000>Counter</color>. When a counter triggers, the defender <color\=#FF0000>ignores the initiative order</color> and uses a <color\=#FF0000>counter technique</color> against the attacker.
Counter techniques do not cost initiative, but they still cost Inner Energy. The chance to counter is determined by the character's <color\=#FF0000>Counter</color> attribute, and the <color\=#FF0000>Counter Damage Bonus</color> attribute adds extra damage when countering.=Counter techniques do not cost initiative, but they still cost Inner Energy. The chance to counter is determined by the character's <color\=#FF0000>Counter</color> attribute, and the <color\=#FF0000>Counter Damage Bonus</color> attribute adds extra damage when countering.
[Counter Range]\nA character's counter reach depends on the <color\=#FF0000>Counter Range</color> attribute. If an enemy attacks from outside counter range, no counter can trigger. In addition, some techniques have <color\=#FF0000>knockback</color> or <color\=#FF0000>pull</color> effects, and a character who is displaced <color\=#FF0000>cannot counter</color> either.=[Counter Range]\nA character's counter reach depends on the <color\=#FF0000>Counter Range</color> attribute. If an enemy attacks from outside counter range, no counter can trigger. In addition, some techniques have <color\=#FF0000>knockback</color> or <color\=#FF0000>pull</color> effects, and a character who is displaced <color\=#FF0000>cannot counter</color> either.
The relevant attributes can be viewed by <color\=#FF0000>right-clicking</color> a unit to open its <color\=#FF0000>character details</color>.=The relevant attributes can be viewed by <color\=#FF0000>right-clicking</color> a unit to open its <color\=#FF0000>character details</color>.

Flaws=Flaws
[Flaws]\nMost characters in the game have <color\=#FF0000>Flaws</color>. When a character attacks from the direction of a <color\=#FF0000>Flaw</color> (the yellow glow), that attack <color\=#FF0000>strikes a Flaw</color>.=[Flaws]\nMost characters in the game have <color\=#FF0000>Flaws</color>. When a character attacks from the direction of a <color\=#FF0000>Flaw</color> (the yellow glow), that attack <color\=#FF0000>strikes a Flaw</color>.
[Striking a Flaw]\nWhen an attack <color\=#FF0000>strikes a Flaw</color>, its <color\=#FF0000>damage</color> and <color\=#FF0000>hit chance</color> are increased, and it reduces the target's <color\=#FF0000>Stance</color> by the technique's <color\=#FF0000>Stance Damage</color>.=[Striking a Flaw]\nWhen an attack <color\=#FF0000>strikes a Flaw</color>, its <color\=#FF0000>damage</color> and <color\=#FF0000>hit chance</color> are increased, and it reduces the target's <color\=#FF0000>Stance</color> by the technique's <color\=#FF0000>Stance Damage</color>.
Note that <color\=#FF0000>Flaws cannot be struck</color> when using a <color\=#FF0000>counter technique</color> or when attacking from a <color\=#FF0000>diagonal</color>.=Note that <color\=#FF0000>Flaws cannot be struck</color> when using a <color\=#FF0000>counter technique</color> or when attacking from a <color\=#FF0000>diagonal</color>.
[Stance]\nUnlike Vitality, when a character's <color\=#FF0000>Stance</color> reaches zero they do not fall at once, but become <color\=#FF0000>Weakened</color>.=[Stance]\nUnlike Vitality, when a character's <color\=#FF0000>Stance</color> reaches zero they do not fall at once, but become <color\=#FF0000>Weakened</color>.
Weakened=Weakened
[Weakened]\nWhen a character becomes <color\=#FF0000>Weakened</color>, they are <color\=#FF0000>pushed back 100 initiative</color>. During this time they <color\=#FF0000>take increased damage</color> and <color\=#FF0000>cannot</color> evade, counter or parry. Weakened <color\=#FF0000>ends when the character's next action begins</color>, restoring all Stance.=[Weakened]\nWhen a character becomes <color\=#FF0000>Weakened</color>, they are <color\=#FF0000>pushed back 100 initiative</color>. During this time they <color\=#FF0000>take increased damage</color> and <color\=#FF0000>cannot</color> evade, counter or parry. Weakened <color\=#FF0000>ends when the character's next action begins</color>, restoring all Stance.

Attribute Points=Attribute Points
[Attribute Points]\nAttribute points can be allocated on the <color\=#FF0000>character screen</color>. There are six attributes, <color\=#FF0000>Strength, Constitution, Inner Force, Agility, Dexterity and Willpower</color>, together called the <color\=#FF0000>Six Attributes</color>.=[Attribute Points]\nAttribute points can be allocated on the <color\=#FF0000>character screen</color>. There are six attributes, <color\=#FF0000>Strength, Constitution, Inner Force, Agility, Dexterity and Willpower</color>, together called the <color\=#FF0000>Six Attributes</color>.
Hover the mouse over an attribute to see a <color\=#FF0000>tooltip</color> explaining its exact effect. Press <color\=#FF0000>V</color> to pin the tooltip.=Hover the mouse over an attribute to see a <color\=#FF0000>tooltip</color> explaining its exact effect. When a character uses a <color\=#FF0000>charged technique</color>, <color\=#FF0000>guide lines</color> and an <color\=#FF0000>attack preview area</color> appear, warning you which units are about to be attacked. When you <color\=#FF0000>lock on</color> to a target with the left button, other characters' preview areas are hidden, helping you tell where a technique comes from.=When a character uses a <color\=#FF0000>charged technique</color>, <color\=#FF0000>guide lines</color> and an <color\=#FF0000>attack preview area</color> appear, warning you which units are about to be attacked. Charged techniques are further divided into <color\=#FF0000>targeted</color> and <color\=#FF0000>untargeted</color> types, distinguished by the <color\=#FF0000>aim</color> icon above the character.=Charged techniques are further divided into <color\=#FF0000>targeted</color> and <color\=#FF0000>untargeted</color> types, distinguished by the <color\=#FF0000>aim</color> icon above the character.
Most charged techniques are <color\=#FF0000>extremely dangerous</color>. [Control Statuses]\nUnlike ordinary abnormal statuses, a character under a <color\=#FF0000>control status</color> loses <color\=#FF0000>control</color> and is forced to carry out specific actions.=[Control Statuses]\nUnlike ordinary abnormal statuses, a character under a <color\=#FF0000>control status</color> loses <color\=#FF0000>control</color> and is forced to carry out specific actions.
The game's <color\=#FF0000>control statuses</color> are <color\=#FF0000>Taunt</color>, <color\=#FF0000>Panic</color> and <color\=#FF0000>Charm</color>. Senior Sister's technique <color\=#FF0000>Soul-Snatching Pill</color> can put a target under <color\=#FF0000>Charm</color>, forcing it over to your side. It is very powerful; do try it.=The game's <color\=#FF0000>control statuses</color> are <color\=#FF0000>Taunt</color>, <color\=#FF0000>Panic</color> and <color\=#FF0000>Charm</color>. Senior Sister's technique <color\=#FF0000>Soul-Snatching Pill</color> can put a target under <color\=#FF0000>Charm</color>, forcing it over to your side. It is very powerful; do try it.
Control statuses <color\=#FF0000>end after the character's action</color>. Note that a character <color\=#FF0000>can carry only one control status at a time</color>, and they <color\=#FF0000>have no effect on Weakened units</color>.=Control statuses <color\=#FF0000>end after the character's action</color>. Note that a character <color\=#FF0000>can carry only one control status at a time</color>, and they <color\=#FF0000>have no effect on Weakened units</color>.

Insight Screen=Insight Screen
[Insight Points]\nThe Insight system is now open. When a character levels up, they now gain <color\=#FF0000>Insight points</color> as well as attribute points, which can be allocated on the Insight screen.=[Insight Points]\nThe Insight system is now open. When a character levels up, they now gain <color\=#FF0000>Insight points</color> as well as attribute points, which can be allocated on the Insight screen.
[Insights]\nEvery hero has their own Insights. Spending Insight points can <color\=#FF0000>raise attributes</color> or grant <color\=#FF0000>battle bonuses</color>. You can <color\=#FF0000>allocate</color> or <color\=#FF0000>refund</color> Insight points at any time; try different combinations to build your character's playstyle.=[Insights]\nEvery hero has their own Insights. Spending Insight points can <color\=#FF0000>raise attributes</color> or grant <color\=#FF0000>battle bonuses</color>. You can <color\=#FF0000>allocate</color> or <color\=#FF0000>refund</color> Insight points at any time; try different combinations to build your character's playstyle.
[Insight Types]\nInsights fall into five types, <color\=#FF0000>Motion, Stillness, Hard, Soft and Finesse</color>, distinguished by color.=[Insight Types]\nInsights fall into five types, <color\=#FF0000>Motion, Stillness, Hard, Soft and Finesse</color>, distinguished by color.
Motion Insights mostly improve mobility, Stillness Insights mostly defense, Hard Insights mostly attack, Soft Insights mostly constitution, and Finesse Insights mostly items and recovery. Use this as a guide when building Insights.=Motion Insights mostly improve mobility, Stillness Insights mostly defense, Hard Insights mostly attack, Soft Insights mostly constitution, and Finesse Insights mostly items and recovery. Use this as a guide when building Insights.
[Inner Arts]\nOnce a character has enough Insight points, they can run an <color\=#FF0000>Inner Art</color>. Different Inner Arts require different types of Insight points to unlock, and <color\=#FF0000>run automatically</color> once there are enough.=[Inner Arts]\nOnce a character has enough Insight points, they can run an <color\=#FF0000>Inner Art</color>. Different Inner Arts require different types of Insight points to unlock, and <color\=#FF0000>run automatically</color> once there are enough.
Inner Art effects are generally very powerful. If you have no clear direction for a character's build, aim to unlock an Inner Art when spending Insight points.=Inner Art effects are generally very powerful. If you have no clear direction for a character's build, aim to unlock an Inner Art when spending Insight points.
Technique Tags=Technique Tags
[Technique Tags]\nTechniques are classed by effect as attack, recovery, buff, debuff and so on. Some Insights and Inner Arts only work on certain types of technique; check them via the <color\=#FF0000>technique tags</color>.=[Technique Tags]\nTechniques are classed by effect as attack, recovery, buff, debuff and so on. Some Insights and Inner Arts only work on certain types of technique; check them via the <color\=#FF0000>technique tags</color>.

[Displacement]\nSome techniques have <color\=#FF0000>knockback</color> or <color\=#FF0000>pull</color> effects, together called <color\=#FF0000>displacement</color>. A displaced character moves in the direction of the attack and <color\=#FF0000>cannot counter</color>. The number of squares moved depends on the technique's <color\=#FF0000>displacement distance</color>.=[Displacement]\nSome techniques have <color\=#FF0000>knockback</color> or <color\=#FF0000>pull</color> effects, together called <color\=#FF0000>displacement</color>. A displaced character moves in the direction of the attack and <color\=#FF0000>cannot counter</color>. The number of squares moved depends on the technique's <color\=#FF0000>displacement distance</color>.
If there is an <color\=#FF0000>enemy unit along the displacement path</color>, that unit launches a <color\=#FF0000>Pursuit</color> against the displaced character.
